# SP99_CW_81 — Changes against witnesses C and H

The supplied scans are the authority. C and H are independent machine drafts. Pure macro differences and the addition of a doubt alone are excluded from change counts. Resolving a C doubt is counted as a reading decision against C, even where its primary candidate is retained. Retained C doubts and witness S decisions are documented with zero change flags where appropriate. Counts refer to editorial places, not token edit distance. Confidence applies to the recorded final reading.

## Consequential corrections

These entries identify changes in formulas, conditions, indices, revision states or diagram structure. Unresolved alternatives are described as potentially consequential; their inclusion here does not certify an uncertain reading.

- Scan 012, Overdrawn large oval at middle-left (012-12): `The complete overdrawn oval, hatching and looping strokes`. Keeps the broad looping overdrawings separate from the bounded hatching; the diagram is not reduced to H's cleaner schematic.
- Scan 012, Entire lower-right drawing ensemble (012-13): `The complete lower-right drawing ensemble`. Restores the omitted lower-right drawing ensemble, including intersecting paths, directional returns and separate oval structures.
- Scan 012, Dark centre of the right lower miniature (012-14): `Filled triangle with pale triangular interior`. Preserves a filled triangular region with a pale triangular interior rather than a generic filled point.
- Scan 015, upper-right NB, second component exponent (015-15): `\Sigma_i^{\unsure{+|-|\ast}}`. The possible positive, negative or star designation selects different components; the crowded exponent remains explicitly unresolved.
- Scan 015, upper-right NB, letter after set difference (015-16): `X\setminus\Sigma_i`. Keeps the written Sigma in the complement although the argument requires E_i; the slip is explained in the mathematical note.
- Scan 015, NB after (b), supposed si (015-18): `\unsure{automatiquement} les`. H's added si changes an unqualified automaticity statement into a conditional one. The needed condition is discussed in the notes but is not supplied to the ink.
- Scan 016, bottom proof, first join under the long closure bar (016-29): `\deleted{\unsure{\amalg}}\cup`. The live join is an ordinary union in a closure calculation, with a doubtful earlier disjoint join preserved as a revision.
- Scan 016, bottom proof, second join under the long closure bar (016-30): `\deleted{\unsure{\amalg}}\cup`. The second live join is likewise an ordinary union; a disjointness assertion is not imposed on the closure expansion.
- Scan 017, First cancelled condition: initial indexed letter (017-01): `E_i`. Preserves E_i as the object in the cancelled condition, rather than substituting the component notation.
- Scan 017, Opening word of the parenthesis (017-06): `(si`. Retains the written conditional si in the hypothesis that introduces the closure identities.
- Scan 017, First join and all indices in the Sigma_12 closure formula (017-11): `\overline{\Sigma_{12}}=\Sigma_{12}\cup E_1\cup E_2`. The closure formula joins the residual region and both separators by ordinary union; the first union doubt is resolved from its rounded strokes.
- Scan 017, Capital Pi in the marginal partition name (017-14): `\Pi_j`. Uses the partition capital Pi in the margin, distinct from the component-set pi notation.
- Scan 017, Raised ring on inserted Pi_J (017-27): `\Pi_J^{\unsure{\circ|0}}`. Preserves the partition marker with circle primary and the recorded zero alternative at its first doubtful occurrence. This is a potentially consequential notation distinction, not a claim that the small round stroke settles its interpretation.
- Scan 017, Transverse bar on the middle indexed union (017-28): `\displaystyle X\setminus\mathop{\overline{\bigcup}}\limits_{j\in J}E_j`. Preserves the independent high transverse on this indexed union; its shape is not silently replaced by a footed disjoint join.
- Scan 017, Ring and two indices on Pi_{J,j} (017-29): `\Pi_{J,j}^{\circ}`. Preserves the partition marker with circle primary and the recorded zero alternative at its first doubtful occurrence. This is a potentially consequential notation distinction, not a claim that the small round stroke settles its interpretation.
- Scan 017, Final quantified letter before membership in J (017-33): `(\unsure{g|j}\in J)`. The literal primary g can be an authorial index slip; j is mathematically expected but remains an alternative because the local upper loop is visible.
- Scan 018, Visible ces n after de/des (018-05): `ces $n$`. Restores the explicit number n of decompositions referred to in the lemma.
- Scan 018, Lemma partition symbol, cancelled subscript and raised ring (018-07): `\Pi_{J\deleted{\unsure{i|j|,}}}^{\unsure{\circ|0}}`. Preserves the changed subscript and round partition marker rather than flattening the revision history.
- Scan 018, First corrected E_i, possible closure bar (018-14): `\unsure{\overline{E_i}|E_i}`. Keeps the alternative between E_i and its closure explicit; the extra high stroke may belong to the earlier correction.
- Scan 018, Second corrected E, subscript j rather than capital J (018-18): `E_j`. Restores the lower-case j index of the other separator rather than a capital index-set J.

This is a potentially consequential notation distinction, not a claim that the small round stroke settles its interpretation.

Preserves the partition marker with circle primary and the recorded zero alternative at its first doubtful occurrence. This is a potentially consequential notation distinction, not a claim that the small round stroke settles its interpretation.
- Scan 024, Overwritten tuple in the lemma introduction (024-09): `$(S,I\unsure{)\deleted{R})|\deleted{)}{,}R)}$`. Keeps the overwritten tuple and unmatched revision states; it does not silently supply H's regular ordered triple.
- Scan 024, What characterises i in condition b) (024-19): `l'ens de ces deux`. The unordered set of the two incident classes determines i; H's l'une would claim determination by one class, a different and generally false assertion.
- Scan 025, Modified union in the main display (025-08): `\mathop{\unsure{\rlap{\raisebox{.65ex}{$\scriptstyle-$}}\bigcup|\deleted{\bigcap}\bigcup}}\limits_{\alpha\in K}`. Keeps the high-cross-stroke/earlier-cap alternatives on the union rather than normalizing the operator from H's note.
- Scan 025, Replacement of the prose-line K by K minus singleton i (025-16): `\deleted{K}\inserted{K\setminus\{i\}}`. Makes the replacement K minus {i} visibly supersede the cancelled K.
- Scan 025, Unfinished set expression and absent right brace (025-21): `K\setminus\{i\}=\bigl\{\alpha\in I\mid E_\alpha\subset\Sigma_\alpha^-`. Leaves the set characterization visibly unfinished. A closing brace would falsely present an incomplete and problematic condition as a completed formula.
- Scan 026, Opening family: alpha below the first Sigma (026-03): `\Sigma_{\alpha}^{\alpha}`. Retains both the lower and upper alpha on the opening family, rather than silently choosing one index position.
- Scan 026, Opening family: index-set glyph after membership (026-04): `\unsure{\mathcal E|\mathscr E|\varepsilon}`. The index set is an E/epsilon-like form rather than omega; its exact alphabet remains uncertain.
- Scan 026, Interlinear clause: lower alpha on its first Sigma (026-10): `\Sigma_{\alpha}^{\alpha}`. Preserves both alpha positions on the first inserted hemisphere symbol.
- Scan 026, Interlinear clause: cancelled lower alpha on second Sigma (026-11): `\Sigma_{\deleted{\unsure{\alpha}}}^{\alpha}`. Records the cancelled lower alpha independently of its live upper replacement.
- Scan 026, Interlinear clause: cancelled lower alpha on final Sigma (026-12): `\Sigma_{\deleted{\unsure{\alpha}}}`. Keeps the old lower index on the last inserted Sigma as a deletion rather than omitting it.
- Scan 026, Interlinear clause: cancelled upper alpha on final Sigma (026-13): `\deleted{\unsure{\alpha}}\alpha,\unsure{0|\circ|o}`. Keeps the separate cancelled upper index beside the live alpha,0 pair.
- Scan 026, Following si condition: cancelled lower alpha (026-18): `\Sigma_{\deleted{\unsure{\alpha}}}^{\alpha}`. Preserves the cancelled lower index in the connectedness condition.
- Scan 026, Cancelled old hypothesis: lower alpha on Sigma (026-20): `\deleted{les $\Sigma_{\alpha}^{\alpha,0}$ connexes}`. Restores the physically written lower alpha in the cancelled hypothesis.
- Scan 026, Replacement condition above the cancelled hypothesis (026-21): `\inserted{$X$ connexe, \deleted{et}}`. Restores the inserted X-connexe hypothesis used by the following boundary argument; H omits this replacement.
- Scan 026, Marginal legend: possible lower alpha on E (026-25): `E_{\unsure{\alpha|{}}}`. The possible alpha subscript of the equator label remains explicit rather than being silently dropped.
- Scan 026, Parenthesis: ne peut être (026-27): `ne peut être`. Restores the negative claim that the pure part cannot be simultaneously open and closed, essential to the connectedness argument.
- Scan 026, Parenthesis: placement of the final car clause (026-30): `car $\Sigma^{\unsure{-,0|\alpha,0|-\alpha,0}}$, est un ouvert $\ne\varnothing$ et $\ne X$)`. Reattaches the nonempty and proper-open predicates to the final car clause, preserving the actual boundary argument.
- Scan 026, Final car clause: first upper sign on Sigma (026-31): `\Sigma^{\unsure{-,0|\alpha,0|-\alpha,0}}`. Keeps the uncertain first superscript with minus primary rather than forcing the general alpha notation used elsewhere.
- Scan 026, Underlined definition phrase (026-37): `\uline{\unsure{non panachées|non penchées|non parallèles|en position}}`. Retains non panachées as the primary definition phrase, with non penchées/non parallèles and the newly admitted en position reading still uncertain.
- Scan 026, First lower inclusion: earlier sign below the live inclusion (026-40): `\mathrel{\unsure{\deleted{\cap}\subset|\subset}}`. Preserves the extra older sign beneath the live inclusion without converting it into a second live relation.

The page has separate light hatches inside the chord region and broad looping zigzags that pass through several regions and extend above and to the right of the rim. H replaces these latter strokes by one bounded patterned area. Their physical paths are restored; cancellation is not imposed as a separate textual state. H text describes the ensemble but the drawing omits it on an editorial cancellation judgment; the ink is preserved here without adopting that judgment. Crop crops/scan012_first_right_ensemble.png; original box [805, 451, 1658, 1185]. Blind audit: The upper framed oval has a small lower-right interruption between its right descending arc and the lower-left return. The first joined them; the revision preserves the gap. A short leftward hook at the lower end of the right interior curve is also visible and is now retained. Crop crops/scan012_second_b18.png; the first and independent readings are recorded in the blind-adjudication table. | medium | H |

### Unresolved places

- upper-right label: `Ok`; alternatives `OK; ok`. The first oval is joined and the k/K upper branch is short; capitalization cannot be fixed by stroke size alone.
- overdrawn middle-left oval: `all original chords, hatches and broad overlying zigzags reproduced`; alternatives `the broad zigzags may cancel the underlying oval`. The broad strokes extend across several regions and beyond the rim; they are graphically distinguishable from hatching, but no separate cancellation word establishes their purpose.
- lower-right framed ensemble: `all framing, oval and crossing strokes reproduced`; alternatives `the whole ensemble may be cancelled, as H’s note asserts`. The framing is heavily retraced, yet the enclosed strokes have coherent directional ends; no unambiguous global strike can be separated from the drawing solely from this leaf.
- isolated middle and lower looped marks: `nonalphabetic pen marks reproduced as drawn`; alternatives `crossed-out single signs`. There are overlapping loops and central strokes but no securely recoverable letter sequence.
- first upper oval right rim: `small gap and detached return retained`; alternatives `very faint continuation across the gap`. The pen becomes faint near the separated short return; the two strong ends remain distinct on the original-resolution half.
- short marks at both edges near y=795: `not transcribed as likely fold-edge artifacts`; alternatives `paired ink ticks`. Both coincide with the fold and meet the paper edge; their image alone does not securely separate dark crease/shadow from ink.

### Blind second reading

Reader blind_a; 23 boxes; 23 token-different boxes; 5 changed after adjudication. Independent second-file SHA-256: `66db5ad1c8e14a5caa88ee651e0c1ad80fa9f36117a9d82babe53987c1cafa84`. Decisions: `records/scan012_audit.json`. The assigned reader did not participate in this page's first reading and received only its images, neutral coordinate boxes and common conventions. No same-page witness, first reading or notes were supplied. The independent TSV was committed and hashed before the scripted token comparison.

### Unresolved places

- column B, overwritten opening of Cascades: `C`; alternatives `Ca`. The second C-shaped curve is distinguishable, but any earlier a merges with the live letter.
- column A, hatched subdivision: `(i)`; alternatives `not independently recoverable`. Two round parentheses survive. A short central upright and a detached spot are consistent with i, but the diagonal hatching obscures their joins.
- column B, hatched subdivision: `(ii)`; alternatives `(i)`. Two short internal downstrokes and separated spots favour ii; hatch strokes cross their upper parts and do not exclude a single i with a cancellation stroke.
- column C, hatched subdivision: `(i)`; alternatives `(ii)`. Round brackets and a central upright are visible; hatching covers the adjacent region where a second i would stand, so i versus ii is not settled.
- column D, hatched subdivision: `(i)`; alternatives `(ii)`. One detached dot and short central stem favour i. The strokes to its left are merged with the diagonal cancellation and could conceal a second i. Keep i first and add the blind reader's ii as an explicit alternative; the added doubt alone is not counted against C.
- column E20, hatched subdivision: `(i)`; alternatives `(ii)`. The enclosing round brackets survive, while hatching obscures the interior and any division into one or two short upright strokes.
- column E23, hatched subdivision: `(ii)`; alternatives `(iv); (iii)`. Two separated upper spots lie above two short descending strokes with rightward feet. These favour ii over the first reading iv; the apparent v-like oblique joins overlap the cancellation strokes. Read ii provisionally, retaining iv and iii as alternatives because the interior cannot be fully separated from the hatching.
- column A, pale label below live (i): `(i)`; alternatives `internal letter not independently secured`. The dark live label has one dotted i between round brackets. A second pale pair of parentheses below it encloses a short faint upright, but its internal detail is not secure enough to remove the doubt.
- column C, pale opening parenthesis at live (i): `(`; alternatives `extent of earlier label undetermined`. A pale extra opening arc lies to the left of the live opening parenthesis. The live label is a dotted i in round brackets; the faint earlier arc does not reveal a complete earlier label.

### Unresolved places

- heading overwritten initial: `I`; alternatives `V`. The live capital and thick crossing strokes do not isolate the prior initial.
- opening family, previous letter: `Σ`; alternatives `S`. The E was written over an angular or curved predecessor; its internal return is obscured.
- condition (a), case of pi: `π_0`; alternatives `Π_0`. A curved top and small left foot weakly favour pi, but its two tall stems nearly match X/E in height; this page has no second pi/Pi exemplar. This is a new uncertainty, not a changed primary reading.
- opening added adjective: `fermés`; alternatives `fermées`. The connected terminal vowel group does not separate one e from two; grammatical agreement does not settle the ink.
- upper-left note, short form: `tte`; alternatives `toute; tt`. Two tall strokes and a compressed final join remain; the complete letter sequence is not separable.
- upper-left note, first Sigma index: `i`; alternatives `no independent index`. The tiny lower stroke may merge with the downturned end of Sigma.
- upper-right NB, second exponent: `+`; alternatives `-; *`. Dense horizontal retracing and vertical protrusions obscure the sign; the expected minus is not substituted silently.
- NB after (b): `automatiquement`; alternatives `none independently supported`. The initial letters are unusually pale.
- case heading, previous group: `(de)`; alternatives `unresolved overwritten group`. Small earlier loops intersect the live (I).
- case heading, previous relation: `≤`; alternatives `=`. The underlying angled stroke is partly covered by the live horizontal pair.
- drawing, crossed top-left label: `Σ_1^+`; alternatives `Σ_2^+`. Cancellation crosses the lower digit.
- drawing, old exponent in right label: `+`; alternatives `unresolved prior sign`. The superscript was blackened and retraced.
- drawing, current exponent in right label: `-`; alternatives `unresolved surviving sign`. A horizontal channel suggests minus but the surrounding oblong remains heavily overdrawn.
- drawing, underlying chord-label letters: `Σ beneath E_1 and E_2`; alternatives `simple retracing of E`. Internal diagonals resemble known corrections on this page, but the small graphic labels are heavily retraced.
- under drawing, struck words: `car si`; alternatives `none independently supported`. Two cancellation strokes obscure the middle letters.
- before symétriquement: `et`; alternatives `unresolved short word`. Only two faint separated traces are visible.
- above lower inclusion, struck words: `vois. ouvert`; alternatives `none independently supported`. The strike covers the connected small letters.
- lower slanted note: `à sign.ler`; alternatives `à signaler`. A point between the n-like run and l may be an abbreviation stop or a reduced a.
- article above fermé: `un`; alternatives `une`. The last join runs into the f region.
- bottom connector: `puis`; alternatives `donc`. The initial has a p-like descender, while subsequent letters are compressed.
- final cancelled group: `Σ_1^+ ≠ ∅`; alternatives `Σ_1^+ serait`. A looped final glyph favours the formula; cancellation leaves the short-letter reading possible.
- upper-left note, before connexe: `d.`; alternatives `unresolved small abbreviation or earlier mark`. The rotated crop shows an extra small loop with an ascending stem and a separate point after est and before connexe. Retain it as doubtful d.; neither the mathematical expectation nor the unexpanded abbreviation licenses omission. The arrow points to the existing label (a), which is not duplicated inside the margin note. The short form after pour and the first component index remain uncertain.
- after (1), replacement phrase: `équivaut à`; alternatives `équiv. à :`. Both cancelled inclusions, the live positive inclusion and the first disjointness formula agree. In the replacement phrase, e with accent, q, the dotted i and a low v-like run are visible, but the tightly compressed ending does not securely distinguish a full équivaut from the independent équiv. proposal. The final two marks resemble the author's tiny à elsewhere, whereas the independent reading takes them as a colon. Reinstate explicit doubt over the whole phrase, with équivaut à first; do not certify either expansion or extra punctuation.

### Unresolved places

- opening corrected subscript: `2 beneath live 1`; alternatives `earlier digit not fully separable`. A small hooked trace survives behind the dark final 1.
- opening overwritten verb: `sera`; alternatives `sont`. The earlier letters merge with the dark live est.
- right-boundary insertion, separator: `/`; alternatives `set-minus`. The short separator is almost upright, so its intended slant is not secure.
- right-boundary insertion, subscript: `2`; alternatives `partly clipped digit`. The low hook reaches the cut edge.
- third opening baseline, cancelled words: `c'est`; alternatives `letters under cancellation not fully recoverable`. The apostrophe and final t survive, but the thick strike obscures the medial letters.
- third opening baseline, live word: `égale`; alternatives `égal`. The low continuation after l may be a separate final e or its exit stroke.
- sign after égale: `:`; alternatives `à`. Two separate points favour a colon; a compressed accented a remains possible.
- connective before opening final inclusion: `donc`; alternatives `d'où`. Only d and a flattened following wave are separately visible.
- opening conclusion abbreviation: `cqfd`; alternatives `qfd`. The first open loop cannot be confidently divided into c and q.
- first partition, letter under Sigma: `E`; alternatives `earlier letter not independently complete`. The additional upright is visible, but a middle E bar is not separate.
- initial letter under Dans: `O`; alternatives `repeated/overwritten D`. Several overlapping loops prevent isolation of a complete earlier capital.
- conditional formula, letter under Sigma 12: `E`; alternatives `earlier letter not independently complete`. Upper and lower retracing survives without a complete separate left stem.
- conditional formula, letter under E 1: `Sigma`; alternatives `earlier letter not independently complete`. An upper diagonal survives but the return diagonal merges with the E bar.
- first earlier join in the bottom proof: `amalg`; alternatives `underlying sign partly merged with live cup`. The lighter basal rule extends past the dark rounded cup; the layering of the uprights remains uncertain.
- second earlier join in the bottom proof: `amalg`; alternatives `underlying sign partly merged with live cup`. A thin straight basal trace and the dark rounded lower strokes overlap.
- closing bracket at the bottom right: `]`; alternatives `upright cut away or merged with border`. Only its high and low short return strokes are independently visible.

### Unresolved places

- first cancelled condition: `soit`; alternatives `est`. Tall terminal stroke and low rounded interior favor soit, but the cancellation merges the central letters.
- first cancelled condition: `rare`; alternatives `dense`. Low outline favors rare; no complete interior sequence survives the heavy strike.
- cancelled sentence before signed equalities: `trouve`; alternatives `unresolved internal letters`. Tall t and shallow following run are visible; all letters cannot be isolated under the rule.
- slanted margin, first word: `introduire`; alternatives `introduisons`. The compressed terminal run favors uire without conclusively excluding isons.
- slanted margin, under E_j: `\Sigma`; alternatives `unresolved underlying angular form`. The diagonal and lower return remain, but E overinks the centre.
- cancelled word after de X: `définie`; alternatives `définies; des parties`. The crossed word has the d and tall f-like stem that favour définie. The terminal low run is merged with the cancellation and does not exclude the second reader’s final s. Add définies as an alternative while retaining the first primary and older alternative.
- first main E_j: `\Sigma`; alternatives `unresolved underlying angular form`. Diagonal under-strokes favor Sigma but its complete earlier path is obscured.
- overwritten beginning before intersection: `et`; alternatives `unresolved earlier letters; H reads an entire cancelled intersection`. Only a compact leading loop and upright can be separated from the overwrite.
- live long word after overwritten beginning: `intersection`; alternatives `unresolved opening letters`. The latter part is recognizable; the start merges with the earlier short word.
- isolated cancelled sign after later E_j: `E`; alternatives `\in`. Two surviving lobes and a diagonal strike admit both signs.
- raised rings on both lower partition names: `\circ`; alternatives `0`. Tiny open rings do not distinguish a circle from handwritten zero; the doubt is marked at the first occurrence only.
- earlier subscript under inserted U_J: `J`; alternatives `j`. Two displaced feet show an overwrite, but the earlier upper stroke is not separable.
- last quantified letter: `g`; alternatives `j`. Closed upper loop favors g-shaped ink; the long hooked j habit leaves j possible. No silent repair is made.
- last word: `P. ex`; alternatives `unresolved short group after P.; previous Pour/Puis rejected as primary`. A separate solid low point follows the capital P; it is not an open o joined into Pour. The following low connected group has a small loop and a long downturned terminal, compatible with ex but not fully separable. Adopt P. followed by uncertain ex, without adding a final point.
- isolated mark at approximately (738,1904): `incidental mark; no semantic token supplied`; alternatives `written dot, possibly \cdot`. Dark short mark has no established textual or mathematical role; root agrees exclusion, with the observation in non_ink_marks and a blind box.
- word after inserted (j in J): `induites`; alternatives `déduites`. The supralinear (j in J) has a long curved leader ending before the baseline word. That word’s first strokes are crowded with the descending j and the leader: a separate dot and low joined run favour induites, but the looped opening also admits déduites. Add the latter as a doubt; do not add a baseline opening parenthesis.

### Unresolved places

- After inserted (unique): `de`; alternatives `des`. The final low return can belong to e or a compressed s; ces n itself is plainly present.
- Lemma Pi subscript after J: `cancelled i`; alternatives `cancelled j; cancelled comma`. The cancellation leaves a dark short down-return without a separable original dot or complete stem.
- Raised mark on Pi in lemma and added class name: `\circ`; alternatives `0`. The raised mark is small and round; ink alone does not distinguish the circle from a small zero. Marked at the first occurrence on the page.
- Subscript of added U and final inclusion U: `\alpha`; alternatives `x`. The low closed/crossed stroke adjoins the return of U; the ending occurrence repeats the compressed shape. Marked at the first occurrence.
- After inserted U_alpha before de: `,`; alternatives `leader bend without independent punctuation`. A short hook occupies the same point as the curved insertion leader’s upper bend; comma is not independent of that stroke.
- Older letter within first E_i correction: `\Sigma`; alternatives `unrecovered earlier capital`. Internal diagonals favour Sigma but part of the old outline is merged with the replacement E; struck-word/symbol doubt retained.
- Extra upper stroke over first corrected E_i: `\overline{E_i}`; alternatives `E_i, with upper stroke belonging to earlier writing`. Two upper horizontals are distinct, but a separate closure bar cannot be fully separated in interpretation from the successive correction strokes. C already includes the barred alternative.
- Older letter within second E_j correction: `\Sigma`; alternatives `unrecovered earlier capital`. Angular internal returns remain but the complete old top stroke is hidden by E.
- Struck word after pi_0(X minus corrected E_j): `choisi`; alternatives `choisie`. The final i dot is visible and no independent e is clear, but the long strike covers part of the letters and terminal area.
- Quoted word at start of baseline 16: `composant`; alternatives `composante`. A t and its hooked exit are clear; a separate final e is not securely distinguishable from that exit.
- Inline pi after du on baseline 17: `\pi_0`; alternatives `\pi`. The low right tail can be the small zero written against the right leg, but unlike the two complete complement formulas it has no independently closed counter. This is a new doubt only, not a changed C/H primary reading.
- Cancelled word beneath added card I=2: `deux`; alternatives `unrecovered word`. Only an initial tall rise and low remnants remain outside the long dense strike; the full four-letter sequence cannot be certified.
- Cancelled group before â/à fortiori: `OK`; alternatives `cela; elle`. The cancelled word under card I=2 remains heavily obscured. The next cancellation has overlapping loops and tall strokes compatible with primary OK, but the second reader’s elle cannot be excluded alongside cela. Add elle as an alternative. The small accent before fortiori remains roof-shaped/bent-grave; the final inclusion and live OK agree.
- Accent on a before fortiori: `â`; alternatives `à`. The extra accent has a roof-like apex but could be a bent start to a grave stroke; the alternatives preserve this graphic ambiguity.
- lower complement in pi_0 formula: `\Sigma_i`; alternatives `\overline{\Sigma_i}`. Cross-page visual comparison during the scan019 audit qualifies the provisional bar reading. The top stroke is detached above a very flat upper diagonal, followed by the lower diagonal and basal arm: this can be the ordinary four-stroke Sigma, as in nearby scan019 formulas. There is no independently secure additional fifth stroke. Plain Sigma is restored as primary; barred Sigma remains an explicit alternative because the compressed separation prompted the blind reader’s bar reading. The letter is still Sigma rather than E. Root personally viewed the complete formula crop and the comparison forms.

### Unresolved places

- Third baseline, before card: `avec`; alternatives `No sufficiently secure second lexical candidate`. Three small detached marks do not separate all four letters.
- First U of paired display: `U with an entrance flourish`; alternatives `\deleted{=}U`. The two horizontal entrance strokes may be an earlier equals sign; their contact with U remains equivocal.
- Below paired display: `l'une`; alternatives `l'un`. The return after n is not separable from a final e; grammatical agreement is not used to decide.
- After Par hyp. de récurrence: `on a`; alternatives `No secure lexical alternative`. Only a flattened oval, short dash and detached point remain.
- Index beneath induction-hypothesis coprod: `\deleted{J}`; alternatives `Earlier letter not securely recoverable`. A returning loop survives beside K, while the upper stroke is obscured by rewriting.
- First brace row, earlier Sigma index: `\alpha`; alternatives `0`. A small loop is entangled with the foot of the revised K.
- Second brace row, component symbol: `\pi_0`; alternatives `\Pi_0`. Roof and two legs support both cases; relative height and curved foot favour lower-case pi.
- Cancelled substrate within initial of On a: `D`; alternatives `O`. A tall inner upright and rounded outer strokes survive, but their earlier sequence cannot be isolated.
- Live phrase before donc: `On a`; alternatives `No secure lexical alternative`. The initial is overdrawn and the n/a are severely contracted; no separate second n supports a whole earlier On.
- Cancelled word after dans la: `somme`; alternatives `réunion`. The horizontal strike masks most letter centres; terminal and initial remnants do not settle the word.
- After se borner: `à`; alternatives `:`. High short mark above a low open curve can be accent plus contracted a or two punctuation components.
- After se borner à: `sommer`; alternatives `No secure lexical alternative`. Initial s-like hook and m-like shoulders are visible, but the o and middle humps are compressed.
- after card I=2, before été: `a`; alternatives `unresolved compressed short mark`. Le has a tall capital L. Between card I=2 and été is a short dark almost horizontal mark, compatible with the compressed a but lacking a separately open bowl. Add uncertainty to that a; the remainder reads été vu. Cas général.

### Unresolved places

- three partition-name superscripts: `\circ`; alternatives `0`. Tiny raised round rings do not distinguish circle from zero; first occurrence marked, later occurrences follow its primary.
- word before parmi: `clas.`; alternatives `deux.`. Open initial curve, tall loop and final point support the abbreviation, but compressed middle leaves deux possible. The separate terminal point applies to either candidate.
- first positive Sigma index: `\deleted{J}i`; alternatives `j; i`. Dotted short return overlies a long left-returning foot whose exact earlier state cannot be separated.
- cancelled Sigma before U: `\Sigma_i`; alternatives `\Sigma_j`. Diagonal crossing strokes merge with the lower indexed return.
- earlier letter in negative barred capital: `E`; alternatives `unresolved earlier form/order`. A left upright survives with the Sigma diagonals, but the overinked junction does not fully certify the earlier form or sequence.
- On ... donc: `sait`; alternatives `voit`. Returning first hook favors s, but local compressed v and a/o alternatives are not excluded.
- cancelled word after live en: `tout`; alternatives `est`. The strike cuts the initial and final rises and hides the middle sequence.
- parenthetic adverb: `aussi`; alternatives `ainsi`. Middle letters are compressed into a nearly straight run; final dotted stroke remains clear.
- earlier sign at current membership: `\Rightarrow`; alternatives `\to; unresolved earlier-arrow state`. Distinct right-pointing angular remnant, but only one shaft is separable from the current membership strokes.

### Unresolved places

- Opening after je dis que: `l'on a`; alternatives `on a`. The broad opening loop and separate low strokes do not isolate a secure l/apostrophe, but do not force its absence either.
- Cancelled second row under K definition: `\Sigma_\alpha`; alternatives `unrecovered cancelled formula`. Angular and indexed remnants survive, but several original strokes merge with dense diagonal cancellations.
- Word following Or: `comme`; alternatives `unrecovered opening letters`. The beginning is retraced and the low middle groups run together; the following on a is independently readable.
- Right-hand Sigma minus in E_i complement equality: `\alpha_0`; alternatives `\alpha`. The low alpha ends in a thick returned tip but has no separately closed zero, unlike the preceding hatted term.
- Lead-in to marginal diagram: `j:e.`; alternatives `j.e.; i.e.`. The initial descends and returns like j; two vertical points follow it. The punctuation is literal primary, while the conventional shorter alternatives remain possible.
- Between sideways signed Sigmas: `upright ! in the page orientation`; alternatives `sideways ou; dash`. The rotated crop shows a point and short line, not two independently identifiable letters. Their orientation makes an upright ! primary, but the intended reading is not fully settled.
- After alpha_0 in K_1, first occurrence of signifi/signifie: `signifi`; alternatives `signifie`. The short stroke by the final dotted i is angled and can be an exit or final e. The second occurrence has a more independent upright terminal stroke and is resolved separately.
- Cancelled word after ce qui: `impli`; alternatives `impl.`. The short word is traversed by a long stroke; the final small upright/point cannot be recovered completely. No que ending is visible.
- Corollary partition exponent: `\circ`; alternatives `0`. The raised ring is round and small; its form does not distinguish a circle from zero.

### Blind second reading

Reader blind_c; 20 boxes; 17 token-different boxes; 0 changed after adjudication. Independent second-file SHA-256: `50b6966f83df78ed021cb35572b9700d45dcdce168f781a53cc08764be713e58`. Decisions: `records/scan021_audit.json`. The assigned reader did not participate in this page's first reading and received only its images, neutral coordinate boxes and common conventions. No same-page witness, first reading or notes were supplied. The independent TSV was committed and hashed before the scripted token comparison.

No transcription changes resulted from this blind reading. All token differences are adjudicated in the cited audit and the corresponding second/*_adjudication.tsv.

### Unresolved places

- Cancelled first word: `On`; alternatives `Un`. The first capital is an open-topped deep bowl; an unclosed O and U remain possible.
- Before alpha-in-K in opening condition: `(`; alternatives `Curve attached to the alpha entrance`. The lower end of the curve joins alpha, preventing secure separation.
- After K at upper-right edge: `)`; alternatives `Clipped parenthesis-like curve`. Only the upper/middle descending curved segment is visible before the paper edge.
- Last word of opening condition: `disjoints`; alternatives `disjointes`. A terminal short return beside the closing parenthesis does not separate e from a pen exit.
- Old locally cancelled relation in second brace row: `\ne`; alternatives `=`. The crossing stroke may belong to the original relation or its cancellation.
- Right vertical relation under main equality: `\deleted{=}`; alternatives `=`. The crossing line and nearby independent Sigma closure bar are close; the parallel ticks remain readable, but their deletion state is not completely secure. No arrowhead supports ⇒.
- Locally cancelled word after Or: `on`; alternatives `No secure second lexical candidate`. An oval and low return are partly hidden by a local strike and later diagonal cancellation.
- Locally cancelled noun below égalité: `formule`; alternatives `No secure second lexical candidate`. Tall f rejects relation, but not every middle letter is independently recoverable under the two cancellations.
- Cancelled space-formula variable: `Y`; alternatives `deleted Z overwritten by Y; Z; X`. Fork, diagonal and lower foot are superposed under heavy retracing; lower strokes do not exclude either alternative. The blind reading supplies a possible two-letter overwrite, but the short transverse remnants can also belong to cancellation; sequence remains unresolved.

### Unresolved places

- First union after X minus V: `\deleted{\bigvee}\bigcup`; alternatives `\bigcup`. The inner oblique and small rising return survive, but an earlier vee cannot be separated securely from a retraced attempt at the cup.
- Old upright inside first hatted Sigma: `I`; alternatives `E`. The interior upright is clear; overlapping upper/lower bars and Sigma diagonals obscure whether an older middle bar existed.
- Cancelled introduction before je dis: `donc si`; alternatives `No safe additional lexical candidate`. The initial looped d and dotted second group survive, but the long strike masks enough intervening letters to retain the doubt.
- Cancelled word in slanted note: `\illegible{1}`; alternatives `No safe candidate`. A black swath and two small end remnants do not yield a word; one word is estimated.
- Index set in slanted note: `I`; alternatives `J`. High horizontal and lower left foot fit I; the sloping hooked termination also permits J.
- Verb after ce qui: `prouvera`; alternatives `prouve`. The longer final trace favours ra, but the reduced final turns prevent resolving C's doubt.
- First separator of lemma hypothesis: `\ne`; alternatives `\deleted{\ne}{,}`. Two bars survive, but an unusually heavy curved descending stroke may be a replacement comma. The second unequal condition remains explicitly written.
- End of cancelled lemma conclusion: `contenu`; alternatives `contenue`. The cancellation crosses the terminal turns; an additional e cannot be certified or excluded.
- Live verb after cancelled signifie que: `provient`; alternatives `précise; précise le`. Retracing merges the low middle strokes and final loop. Context alone is not used to choose among them.
- Last short word before Corollaire: `du`; alternatives `des; sens; donc`. A raised first loop and flattened following turns allow a partial reading but not a secure ending. The blind reader’s donc also fits the compressed low turns, but its ending is not secure.
- Final qualifier of parenthesized family: `K`; alternatives `I`. The forked strokes favour K, but the crossed/retraced upright remains ambiguous.
- High ring on Pi_K: `\circ`; alternatives `0`. The tiny closed ring has no locally decisive distinction between a circle marker and zero.
- Transition to lemma: verb: `revient`; alternatives `mérite`. Several low peaks, the detached dot and reduced final return do not settle the compressed middle.
- Transition to lemma: final short group: `au`; alternatives `un`. The group lacks a d ascender, but its flattened low turns do not settle au versus un; the earlier resolution is withdrawn.
- Start of unfinished transition: `Ce $L^\circ$`; alternatives `Ce $L^o$; Cela`. A separated upright with a rightward foot and a small raised curved mark differs from the connected looped l and baseline a in b10.

### Unresolved places

- Opening partition exponent: `\circ`; alternatives `0`. The high small closed circle has no distinguishing stroke between a circle mark and a zero; retain both.
- Cancelled words after (s in S): `On a`; alternatives `On`. The terminal body may be a but lies under cancellation; not every letter is plain.
- Deleted glyph before (s,i): `U`; alternatives `E`. Dense intersecting strokes preserve both a low return and an upright/cross-stroke pattern.
- Overwritten tuple after Cette relation fait de: `(S,I)\deleted{R})`; alternatives `(S,I\deleted{)}{,}R)`. The first closing curve is visible, but crossings over the R region prevent secure recovery of the revision order.
- Before sommets: `ens des`; alternatives `ens de`. The connected terminal run admits either a final s or merely the exit from e; the period after ens is absent.
- Before arêtes: `de`; alternatives `d'`. A small loop connected to the exit from d favours e; an unusually looped d exit cannot be excluded. Both witnesses had d'.
- Word for edges: `arêtes`; alternatives `arètes; aretes`. The high trace overlaps the r/e/t group and may belong to the tall t rather than an independent accent.
- After cancelled Cette relation: `Le`; alternatives `Ce`. The upright and square foot favour L, but a detached upper curve admits C.
- Cancelled word after Il: `existe`; alternatives `ex`. Only low letter remnants and a rising terminal hook survive clearly under the horizontal cancellation.
- Beginning of signed-component paragraph: `Soient`; alternatives `Soit`. The low final run ends in a looped rise without a securely separable n.
- After comp.: `connexes`; alternatives `compressed interior letters not individually secure`. Three low groups occupy the full word, with no stop as in H conn.; the letter sequence remains compressed.
- Before underlined un: `exactement`; alternatives `exactemt; existe`. A low run and crossed triangular ending do not securely distinguish the full ending from the shorter form. The blind reading existe also fits the compressed middle and crossed ending.
- First chosen U, sign following s: `+`; alternatives `-`. The saturated patch has no independently identifiable vertical arm. The exposition suggests the positive choice but does not supply a stroke.
- First containing Sigma, raised sign: `+`; alternatives `-`. The thick horizontal patch can conceal or lack a vertical arm. Preserve the ink doubt separately from the expected positive designation.
- Right edge after the negative Sigma and comma: `dé`; alternatives `qui`. Two looped rising strokes overlap and do not securely settle the word.
- Verb starting the last main baseline: `définit`; alternatives `definit`. The existing final-line crop does not isolate an acute accent above e. Both witnesses retain the accented primary; new uncertainty alone is not a correction.
- Sign beneath s in the positive closure intersection: `+`; alternatives `-`. A saturated small patch and low protrusion suggest plus but do not isolate a vertical arm; mathematical expectation does not resolve the ink.

### Unresolved places

- Earlier letter within opening Sigma: `\deleted{E}`; alternatives `\deleted{I}`. The older upright and horizontal ends overlap the live Sigma; the discriminating middle bar is not separable.
- Opening E subscript: `i`; alternatives `j`. A point and a short curved body are present, but the body is too small to distinguish local i from j.
- Small isolated continuation before main display: `on a`; alternatives `No defensible alternative word sequence; retain the preferred phrase as unsure.`. Three heavily contracted bodies do not support a secure independent letter-by-letter reading.
- Operator after X difference in first display: `\rlap{\raisebox{.65ex}{$\scriptstyle-$}}\bigcup`; alternatives `\deleted{\bigcap}\bigcup`. A high left cross-stroke is certain, but the doubled bowl does not reveal the order of the inner strokes.
- Cancelled word after pi expression at right edge: `les`; alternatives `K; tel; et`. The tall loop and subsequent strokes share a cancellation and edge crowding. A separate terminal comma is not distinguishable. The central low loop and final crossed upright also permit et; the primary remains les.
- Cancelled shapes before fresh les: `U\Sigma`; alternatives `\bigcup\Sigma; U`. The angular return lies inside the bowl, but stroke order and the exact earlier letter or operator are obscured.
- Final word of mutual-disjointness condition: `disjoints`; alternatives `Clipped terminal shape; no secure different word proposed.`. The final strokes reach the photographed right edge; the full terminal shape and any following stop are unavailable.

### Unresolved places

- Opening family index-set glyph: `\mathcal E`; alternatives `\mathscr E; \varepsilon`. Two vertically arranged lobes and a lower sweep are visible, but handwritten font and case do not decide among these forms.
- Second interlinear Sigma: cancelled lower glyph: `\alpha`; alternatives `No safe further symbol candidate`. A lower loop and hooked tail survive through a dark cancellation; the middle is filled.
- Final interlinear Sigma: cancelled lower glyph: `\alpha`; alternatives `No safe further symbol candidate`. The surviving low corner and fork fit alpha, but the crossed centre is opaque.
- Final interlinear Sigma: cancelled upper glyph: `\alpha`; alternatives `No safe further symbol candidate`. Only an angled/curled remnant of the separate high cancelled mark survives.
- First upper ring after alpha and comma: `0`; alternatives `\circ; o`. The little open ring is at superscript height and gives no decisive local distinction; the doubt is marked only at its first occurrence.
- Cancelled token after connexes: `$\Sigma_{\alpha}$`; alternatives `les`. The cancellation covers both the angular upper form and low part, leaving the symbol/word distinction unresolved.
- Sigma after si les: cancelled lower glyph: `\alpha`; alternatives `No safe further symbol candidate`. The blacked lower mark has a short hook but no fully separated body.
- Second E-empty relation: `\ne`; alternatives `+`. The long lower bar and crossing slant are clear; the short upper remnant does not securely decide unequal versus plus.
- Word after on conclut and its cancellation state: `\unsure{\deleted{aussitôt}}`; alternatives `Word reading uncertain; deletion is primary but cancellation intent is also uncertain.`. The opening au and two high t-like strokes support aussitôt, but the middle letters remain merged. The long low stroke may be a cancellation, yet it closely follows the letter baseline and has no decisive independent extension beyond the word. Keep the deleted reading as primary while extending the uncertainty to cancellation intent as well as the word.
- Cancelled upper label of marginal segment: `E`; alternatives `\Sigma; B`. Upright, middle bar and right-hand diagonal/loop remnants overlap with the cancellation.
- Lower right of E in marginal legend: `\alpha subscript`; alternatives `No subscript`. Small loop and fork touch the bottom bar of E and may be a terminal pen return.
- Final car clause: Sigma upper pair: `-,0`; alternatives `\alpha,0; -\alpha,0`. The first high stroke is nearly flat; a compressed loop or extra alpha cannot be excluded.
- Second binary pair opening: `[`; alternatives `(`. A flat upper serif and nearly upright stem favour a bracket; the rounded lower return retains a parenthesis alternative. The right brace is not repaired.
- Cancelled distinctes after the pairs: `distinctes`; alternatives `No safe further word candidate`. Upper/end letter remnants resemble the clear inserted occurrence, but several middle letters remain crossed.
- Underlined definition phrase: `non panachées`; alternatives `non penchées; non parallèles; en position`. The first short group favours three reduced strokes non. The second has a deep looped p, a small round return, low middle strokes, a late tall stem and a high short mark. These still favour non panachées, with the older alternatives retained. The compressed middle and late marks also admit the independent en position segmentation, so add that full-phrase alternative without changing the primary. The line below is an underline; the p descender is not a strike.
- First lower inclusion: older sign: `\deleted{\cap}\subset`; alternatives `\subset`. An extra lower-left prong and interior return survive, but an earlier cap cannot be certified.
- Second equality label: `i.e.`; alternatives `ie`. The i dot is clear, but the other possible stops merge with the joining stroke and terminal return.
- a in on a alors: `a`; alternatives `No further letter candidate; mark too reduced for certainty.`. The final word has a small low entry followed by a tall looped l and an extended low ending, favouring alors rather than donc. The isolated mark between on and alors is too reduced to distinguish the a body confidently. Retain a as primary but mark it uncertain; do not change alors.
